1. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
2. 
3. using System;
4. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
5. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
6. using Windows.Foundation;
7. using Windows.UI.Core;
8. 
9. namespace SDKTemplate
10. {
11.     /// <summary>
12.     /// Updates an ObservableCollection based on events from a DeviceWatcher.
13.     /// </summary>
14.     /// <remarks>
15.     /// Encapsulates the work necessary to register for watcher events,
16.     /// start and stop the watcher, handle race conditions, and break cycles.
17.     /// </remarks>
18.     class DeviceWatcherHelper
19.     {
20.         public DeviceWatcherHelper(
21.             ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection,
22.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
23.             CoreDispatcher dispatcher)
24.         {
25.             this.resultCollection = resultCollection;
26.             this.dispatcher = dispatcher;
27.         }
28. 
29.         public delegate void DeviceChangedHandler(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher, string id);
30.         public event DeviceChangedHandler DeviceChanged;
31. 
32.         public DeviceWatcher DeviceWatcher => deviceWatcher;
33.         public bool UpdateStatus = true;
34. 
35.         public void StartWatcher(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher)
36.         {
37.             this.deviceWatcher = deviceWatcher;
38. 
39.             // Connect events to update our collection as the watcher report results.
40.             deviceWatcher.Added += Watcher_DeviceAdded;
41.             deviceWatcher.Updated += Watcher_DeviceUpdated;
42.             deviceWatcher.Removed += Watcher_DeviceRemoved;
43.             deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted += Watcher_EnumerationCompleted;
44.             deviceWatcher.Stopped += Watcher_Stopped;
45. 
46.             deviceWatcher.Start();
47.         }
48. 
49.         public void StopWatcher()
50.         {
51.             // Since the device watcher runs in the background, it is possible that
52.             // a notification is "in flight" at the time we stop the watcher.
53.             // In other words, it is possible for the watcher to become stopped while a
54.             // handler is running, or for a handler to run after the watcher has stopped.
55. 
56.             if (IsWatcherStarted(deviceWatcher))
57.             {
58.                 // We do not null out the deviceWatcher yet because we want to receive
59.                 // the Stopped event.
60.                 deviceWatcher.Stop();
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         public void Reset()
65.         {
66.             if (deviceWatcher != null)
67.             {
68.                 StopWatcher();
69.                 deviceWatcher = null;
70.             }
71.         }
72. 
73.         DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
74.         ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection;
75.         // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
76.         CoreDispatcher dispatcher;
77. 
78.         static bool IsWatcherStarted(DeviceWatcher watcher)
79.         {
80.             return (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
81.                 (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted);
82.         }
83. 
84.         public bool IsWatcherRunning()
85.         {
86.             if (deviceWatcher == null)
87.             {
88.                 return false;
89.             }
90. 
91.             DeviceWatcherStatus status = deviceWatcher.Status;
92.             return (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
93.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted) ||
94.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopping);
95.         }
96. 
97.         private async void Watcher_DeviceAdded(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformation deviceInfo)
98.         {
99.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
100.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
101.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
102.             {
103.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
104.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
105.                 {
106.                     resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(deviceInfo));
107.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfo.Id);
108.                 }
109.             });
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void Watcher_DeviceUpdated(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
113.         {
114.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
115.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
116.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
117.             {
118.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
119.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
120.                 {
121.                     // Find the corresponding updated DeviceInformation in the collection and pass the update object
122.                     // to the Update method of the existing DeviceInformation. This automatically updates the object
123.                     // for us.
124.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
125.                     {
126.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
127.                         {
128.                             deviceInfoDisp.Update(deviceInfoUpdate);
129.                             RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
130.                             break;
131.                         }
132.                     }
133.                 }
134.             });
135.         }
136. 
137.         private async void Watcher_DeviceRemoved(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
138.         {
139.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
140.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
141.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
142.             {
143.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
144.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
145.                 {
146.                     // Find the corresponding DeviceInformation in the collection and remove it
147.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
148.                     {
149.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
150.                         {
151.                             resultCollection.Remove(deviceInfoDisp);
152.                             break;
153.                         }
154.                     }
155. 
156.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
157.                 }
158.             });
159.         }
160. 
161.         private async void Watcher_EnumerationCompleted(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
162.         {
163.             // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
164.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
165.             {
166.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
167.             });
168.         }
169. 
170.         private async void Watcher_Stopped(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
171.         {
172.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
173.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
174.             {
175.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
176.             });
177.         }
178. 
179.         private void RaiseDeviceChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
180.         {
181.             if (UpdateStatus)
182.             {
183.                 // Display a default status message.
184.                 string message = string.Empty;
185.                 switch (sender.Status)
186.                 {
187.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Started:
188.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found.";
189.                         break;
190. 
191.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted:
192.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Enumeration completed. Watching for updates...";
193.                         break;
194. 
195.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopped:
196.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher stopped.";
197.                         break;
198. 
199.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Aborted:
200.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher aborted.";
201.                         break;
202.                 }
203. 
204.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(message))
205.                 {
206.                     MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(message, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
207.                 }
208.             }
209. 
210.             DeviceChanged?.Invoke(sender, id);
211.         }
212.     }
213. }

21.     {
22.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
23. 
24.         private ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection = new ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay>();
25. 
26.         public Scenario1_DevicePicker()
27.         {
28.             this.InitializeComponent();
29.         }
30. 
31.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
32.         {
33.             resultsListView.ItemsSource = resultCollection;
34.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.DevicePickerSelectors;
35.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
36.         }

39.         {
40.             ShowDevicePicker(
41.                 true // pickSingle
42.                 );
43.         }

46.         {
47.             ShowDevicePicker(
48.                 false // pickSingle
49.                 );
50.         }

52.         private async void ShowDevicePicker(bool pickSingle)
53.         {
54.             showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = false;
55.             resultCollection.Clear();
56. 
57.             var devicePicker = new DevicePicker();
58. 
59.             // First get the device selector chosen by the UI.
60.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
61. 
62.             if (null == deviceSelectorInfo.Selector)
63.             {
64.                 // If the a pre-canned device class selector was chosen, call the DeviceClass overload
65.                 devicePicker.Filter.SupportedDeviceClasses.Add(deviceSelectorInfo.DeviceClassSelector);
66.             }
67.             else
68.             {
69.                 // Use AQS string selector from dynamic call to a device api's GetDeviceSelector call
70.                 devicePicker.Filter.SupportedDeviceSelectors.Add(deviceSelectorInfo.Selector);
71.             }
72. 
73.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Showing Device Picker", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             // Calculate the position to show the picker (right below the buttons)
76.             GeneralTransform ge = pickSingleDeviceButton.TransformToVisual(null);
77.             Point point = ge.TransformPoint(new Point());
78.             Rect rect = new Rect(point, new Point(point.X + pickSingleDeviceButton.ActualWidth, point.Y + pickSingleDeviceButton.ActualHeight));
79. 
80.             if (pickSingle)
81.             {
82.                 DeviceInformation di = await devicePicker.PickSingleDeviceAsync(rect);
83.                 if (null != di)
84.                 {
85.                     resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(di));
86.                 }
87.                 showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = true;
88.             }
89.             else
90.             {
91.                 devicePicker.DevicePickerDismissed += new TypedEventHandler<DevicePicker, object>(
92.                 async (picker, obj) =>
93.                 {
94.                     // Must access UI elements from the UI thread.
95.                     // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
96.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
97.                     {
98.                         showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = true;
99.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Hiding Device Picker", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
100.                     });
101.                 });
102. 
103.                 devicePicker.DeviceSelected += new TypedEventHandler<DevicePicker, DeviceSelectedEventArgs>(
104.                 async (picker, args) =>
105.                 {
106.                     // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
107.                     // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
108.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
109.                     {
110.                         resultCollection.Clear();
111.                         resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(args.SelectedDevice));
112.                     });
113.                 });
114. 
115.                 // Show the picker
116.                 devicePicker.Show(rect);
117.             }
118.         }
119.     }

21.     public sealed partial class Scenario9_CustomPairDevice : Page
22.     {
23.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
24.

26. 
27.         TaskCompletionSource<string> providePinTaskSrc;
28.         TaskCompletionSource<bool> confirmPinTaskSrc;
29.         TaskCompletionSource<PasswordCredential> providePasswordCredential;

34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36.

38.             deviceWatcherHelper.DeviceChanged += OnDeviceListChanged;
39.         }

42.         {

44. 
45.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.PairingSelectors;
46.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
47.

49.             protectionLevelComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;

55.             CompleteProvidePinTask(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
56.             CompletePasswordCredential(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
57.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(false); // Abandon any previous request.

70.         private void StartWatcher()
71.         {
72.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
73.             resultCollection.Clear();
74.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher;
75. 
76.             // Get the device selector chosen by the UI then add additional constraints for devices that
77.             // can be paired or are already paired.
78.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
79.             string selector = "(" + deviceSelectorInfo.Selector + ")" + " AND (System.Devices.Aep.CanPair:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True OR System.Devices.Aep.IsPaired:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True)";
80. 
81.             if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
82.             {
83.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
84.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
85.                     selector,
86.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
87.                     );
88.             }
89.             else
90.             {
91.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
92.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
93.                     selector,
94.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
95.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
96.             }
97. 
98.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
99.             deviceWatcherHelper.StartWatcher(deviceWatcher);
100.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
101.         }

104.         {
105.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
106.             deviceWatcherHelper.StopWatcher();
107.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
108.         }

110.         private void OnDeviceListChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
111.         {

113.             DeviceInformationDisplay selectedDeviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;
114.             if ((selectedDeviceInfoDisp != null) && (selectedDeviceInfoDisp.Id == id))
115.             {
116.                 UpdatePairingButtons();
117.             }

120.         private async void PairButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
121.         {
122.             // Gray out the pair button and results view while pairing is in progress.
123.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
124.             pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
125.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Pairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
126. 
127.             // Get the device selected for pairing
128.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
129. 
130.             // Get ceremony type and protection level selections
131.             DevicePairingKinds ceremoniesSelected = GetSelectedCeremonies();
132.             ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo protectionLevelInfo = (ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo)protectionLevelComboBox.SelectedItem;
133.             DevicePairingProtectionLevel protectionLevel = protectionLevelInfo.ProtectionLevel;
134. 
135.             DeviceInformationCustomPairing customPairing = deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.Custom;
136. 
137.             customPairing.PairingRequested += PairingRequestedHandler;
138.             DevicePairingResult result = await customPairing.PairAsync(ceremoniesSelected, protectionLevel);
139.             customPairing.PairingRequested -= PairingRequestedHandler;
140. 
141.             rootPage.NotifyUser(
142.                 "Pairing result = " + result.Status.ToString(),
143.                 result.Status == DevicePairingResultStatus.Paired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
144. 
145.             HidePairingPanel();
146.             UpdatePairingButtons();
147.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;
148.         }

150.         private async void UnpairButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
151.         {
152.             // Gray out the unpair button and results view while unpairing is in progress.
153.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
154.             unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;
155.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Unpairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
156. 
157.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
158.

160.

162.                 "Unpairing result = " + dupr.Status.ToString(),
163.                 dupr.Status == DeviceUnpairingResultStatus.Unpaired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
164. 
165.             UpdatePairingButtons();
166.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;
167.         }
168. 
169.         private async void PairingRequestedHandler(
170.             DeviceInformationCustomPairing sender,
171.             DevicePairingRequestedEventArgs args)
172.         {
173.             switch (args.PairingKind)
174.             {
175.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly:
176.                     // Windows itself will pop the confirmation dialog as part of "consent" if this is running on Desktop or Mobile
177.                     // If this is an App for 'Windows IoT Core' where there is no Windows Consent UX, you may want to provide your own confirmation.
178.                     args.Accept();
179.                     break;
180. 
181.                 case DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin:
182.                     // We just show the PIN on this side. The ceremony is actually completed when the user enters the PIN
183.                     // on the target device. We automatically accept here since we can't really "cancel" the operation
184.                     // from this side.
185.                     args.Accept();
186. 
187.                     // No need for a deferral since we don't need any decision from the user
188.                     // TODO[migrate-013]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
189.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
190.                     {
191.                         ShowPairingPanel(
192.                             "Please enter this PIN on the device you are pairing with: " + args.Pin,
193.                             args.PairingKind);
194. 
195.                     });
196.                     break;
197. 
198.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin:
199.                     // A PIN may be shown on the target device and the user needs to enter the matching PIN on
200.                     // this Windows device. Get a deferral so we can perform the async request to the user.
201.                     var collectPinDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
202. 
203.                     // TODO[migrate-014]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
204.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
205.                     {
206.                         string pin = await GetPinFromUserAsync();
207.                         if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(pin))
208.                         {
209.                             args.Accept(pin);
210.                         }
211. 
212.                         collectPinDeferral.Complete();
213.                     });
214.                     break;
215. 
216.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential:
217.                     var collectCredentialDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
218.                     // TODO[migrate-015]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
219.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
220.                     {
221.                         var credential = await GetPasswordCredentialFromUserAsync();
222.                         if (credential != null)
223.                         {
224.                             args.AcceptWithPasswordCredential(credential);
225.                         }
226.                         collectCredentialDeferral.Complete();
227.                     });
228.                     break;
229. 
230.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch:
231.                     // We show the PIN here and the user responds with whether the PIN matches what they see
232.                     // on the target device. Response comes back and we set it on the PinComparePairingRequestedData
233.                     // then complete the deferral.
234.                     var displayMessageDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
235. 
236.                     // TODO[migrate-016]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
237.                     await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
238.                     {
239.                         bool accept = await GetUserConfirmationAsync(args.Pin);
240.                         if (accept)
241.                         {
242.                             args.Accept();
243.                         }
244. 
245.                         displayMessageDeferral.Complete();
246.                     });
247.                     break;
248.             }
249.         }
250. 
251.         private void ShowPairingPanel(string text, DevicePairingKinds pairingKind)
252.         {
253.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
254.             pinEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
255.             okButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
256.             usernameEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
257.             passwordEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
258.             verifyButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
259.             yesButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
260.             noButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
261.             pairingTextBlock.Text = text;
262. 
263.             switch (pairingKind)
264.             {
265.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly:
266.                 case DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin:
267.                     // Don't need any buttons
268.                     break;
269.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin:
270.                     pinEntryTextBox.Text = "";
271.                     pinEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
272.                     okButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
273.                     break;
274.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch:
275.                     yesButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
276.                     noButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
277.                     break;
278.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential:
279.                     usernameEntryTextBox.Text = "";
280.                     passwordEntryTextBox.Text = "";
281.                     passwordEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
282.                     usernameEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
283.                     verifyButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
284.                     break;
285.             }
286. 
287.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
288.         }
289. 
290.         private void HidePairingPanel()
291.         {
292.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
293.             pairingTextBlock.Text = "";
294.         }
295. 
296.         private async Task<string> GetPinFromUserAsync()
297.         {
298.             HidePairingPanel();
299.             CompleteProvidePinTask(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
300. 
301.             ShowPairingPanel(
302.                 "Please enter the PIN shown on the device you're pairing with",
303.                 DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin);
304. 
305.             providePinTaskSrc = new TaskCompletionSource<string>();
306. 
307.             return await providePinTaskSrc.Task;
308.         }
309. 
310.         // If pin is not provided, then any pending pairing request is abandoned.
311.         private void CompleteProvidePinTask(string pin = null)
312.         {
313.             if (providePinTaskSrc != null)
314.             {
315.                 providePinTaskSrc.SetResult(pin);
316.                 providePinTaskSrc = null;
317.             }
318.         }
319. 
320.         private async Task<PasswordCredential> GetPasswordCredentialFromUserAsync()
321.         {
322.             HidePairingPanel();
323.             CompletePasswordCredential(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
324. 
325.             ShowPairingPanel(
326.                 "Please enter the username and password",
327.                 DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential);
328. 
329.             providePasswordCredential = new TaskCompletionSource<PasswordCredential>();
330. 
331.             return await providePasswordCredential.Task;
332.         }
333. 
334.         private void CompletePasswordCredential(string username = null, string password = null)
335.         {
336.             if (providePasswordCredential != null)
337.             {
338.                 if (String.IsNullOrEmpty(username))
339.                 {
340.                     providePasswordCredential.SetResult(null);
341.                 }
342.                 else
343.                 {
344.                     providePasswordCredential.SetResult(new PasswordCredential() { UserName = username, Password = password });
345.                 }
346.                 providePasswordCredential = null;
347.             }
348.         }
349. 
350.         private async Task<bool> GetUserConfirmationAsync(string pin)
351.         {
352.             HidePairingPanel();
353.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(false); // Abandon any previous request.
354. 
355.             ShowPairingPanel(
356.                 "Does the following PIN match the one shown on the device you are pairing?: " + pin,
357.                 DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch);
358. 
359.             confirmPinTaskSrc = new TaskCompletionSource<bool>();
360. 
361.             return await confirmPinTaskSrc.Task;
362.         }
363. 
364.         // If pin is not provided, then any pending pairing request is abandoned.
365.         private void CompleteConfirmPinTask(bool accept)
366.         {
367.             if (confirmPinTaskSrc != null)
368.             {
369.                 confirmPinTaskSrc.SetResult(accept);
370.                 confirmPinTaskSrc = null;
371.             }
372.         }
373.

378.             HidePairingPanel();
379.         }

385.             HidePairingPanel();
386.         }

392.         }

395.         {
396.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(false);
397.             HidePairingPanel();
398.         }
399. 
400.         private DevicePairingKinds GetSelectedCeremonies()
401.         {
402.             DevicePairingKinds ceremonySelection = DevicePairingKinds.None;
403.             if (confirmOnlyOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly;
404.             if (displayPinOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin;
405.             if (providePinOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin;
406.             if (confirmPinMatchOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch;
407.             if (passwordCredentialOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential;
408.             return ceremonySelection;
409.         }

411.         private void ResultsListView_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
412.         {
413.             UpdatePairingButtons();
414.         }
415. 
416.         private void UpdatePairingButtons()
417.         {
418.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;
419. 
420.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
421.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.CanPair &&
422.                 !deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
423.             {
424.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = true;
425.             }
426.             else
427.             {
428.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
429.             }
430. 
431.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
432.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
433.             {
434.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = true;
435.             }
436.             else
437.             {
438.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;
439.             }
440.         }
441.     }

1. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
2. 
3. using System;
4. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Background;
5. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
6. using Windows.Storage;
7. using Windows.UI.Core;
8. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml;
9. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;
10. using Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
11. 
12. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=234238
13. 
14. namespace SDKTemplate
15. {
16.     /// <summary>
17.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
18.     /// </summary>
19.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_BackgroundDeviceWatcher : Page
20.     {
21.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
22. 
23.         private string backgroundTaskName = "DeviceEnumerationCs_BackgroundTaskName";
24.         private IBackgroundTaskRegistration backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
25. 
26.         public Scenario3_BackgroundDeviceWatcher()
27.         {
28.             this.InitializeComponent();
29.         }
30. 
31.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
32.         {
33.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.BackgroundDeviceWatcherSelectors;
34.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
35. 
36.             // Determine if the background task is already active
37.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
38.             {
39.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
40.                 {
41.                     backgroundTaskRegistration = task.Value;
42.                     startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
43.                     stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             }
47.         }
48. 
49.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
50.         {
51.             if (backgroundTaskRegistration != null)
52.             {
53.                 backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed -= OnTaskCompleted;
54.                 backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
55.             }
56.         }
57. 
58.         private void StartWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
59.         {
60.             StartWatcher();
61.         }
62. 
63.         private void StopWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
64.         {
65.             StopWatcher();
66.         }
67. 
68.         private void StartWatcher()
69.         {
70.             DeviceWatcherEventKind[] triggerEventKinds = { DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update };
71.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
72. 
73.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
74. 
75.             // First get the device selector chosen by the UI.
76.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
77. 
78.             if (null == deviceSelectorInfo.Selector)
79.             {
80.                 // If the a pre-canned device class selector was chosen, call the DeviceClass overload
81.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(deviceSelectorInfo.DeviceClassSelector);
82.             }
83.             else if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
84.             {
85.                 // Use AQS string selector from dynamic call to a device api's GetDeviceSelector call
86.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
87.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
88.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
89.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
90.                     );
91.             }
92.             else
93.             {
94.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
95.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
96.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
97.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
98.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
99.             }
100. 
101.             // Get the background trigger for this watcher
102.             DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger = deviceWatcher.GetBackgroundTrigger(triggerEventKinds);
103. 
104.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
105. 
106.             // Register this trigger for our background task
107.             RegisterBackgroundTask(deviceWatcherTrigger);
108. 
109.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
110.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Watcher started...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
111.         }
112. 
113.         private void RegisterBackgroundTask(DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger)
114.         {
115.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
116.             BackgroundTaskBuilder taskBuilder;
117. 
118.             // First see if we already have this background task registered. If so, unregister
119.             // it before we register it with the new trigger.
120. 
121.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
122.             {
123.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
124.                 {
125.                     UnregisterBackgroundTask(task.Value);
126.                 }
127.             }
128. 
129.             // TODO[migrate-011]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
130.             taskBuilder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
131.             taskBuilder.Name = backgroundTaskName;
132.             taskBuilder.TaskEntryPoint = "BackgroundDeviceWatcherTaskCs.BackgroundDeviceWatcher";
133.             taskBuilder.SetTrigger(deviceWatcherTrigger);
134. 
135.             backgroundTaskRegistration = taskBuilder.Register();
136.             backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed += OnTaskCompleted;
137.         }
138. 
139.         private async void OnTaskCompleted(BackgroundTaskRegistration task, BackgroundTaskCompletedEventArgs args)
140.         {
141.             // TODO[migrate-012]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
142.             await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
143.             {
144.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task completed", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
145.             });
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void StopWatcher()
149.         {
150.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
151.             if (null != backgroundTaskRegistration)
152.             {
153.                 UnregisterBackgroundTask(backgroundTaskRegistration);
154.             }
155.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
156.         }
157. 
158.         private void UnregisterBackgroundTask(IBackgroundTaskRegistration taskReg)
159.         {
160.             taskReg.Unregister(true);
161.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
162.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = (uint)0;
163.         }
164.     }
165. }
166.

4. using System.Collections.Generic;

9. using System.Diagnostics;

15. namespace BackgroundDeviceWatcherTaskCs
16. {
17.     // TODO[migrate-017]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
18.     public sealed class BackgroundDeviceWatcher : IBackgroundTask
19.     {
20.         public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
21.         {
22.             Debug.WriteLine("BackgroundDeviceWatcher.Run called");
23. 
24.             uint eventCount = 0;
25.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
26.             DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails triggerDetails = (DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
27. 
28.             Debug.WriteLine(String.Format("Trigger contains {0} events", triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count));
29. 
30.             foreach (DeviceWatcherEvent e in triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents)
31.             {
32.                 switch (e.Kind)
33.                 {
34.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add:
35.                     Debug.WriteLine("Add: " + e.DeviceInformation.Id);
36.                     break;
37. 
38.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update:
39.                     Debug.WriteLine("Update: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
40.                     break;
41. 
42.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove:
43.                     Debug.WriteLine("Remove: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             };
47.             
48.             if (null != settings.Values["eventCount"] &&
49.                 settings.Values["eventCount"].GetType() == typeof(uint))
50.             {
51.                 eventCount = (uint)settings.Values["eventCount"];
52.             }
53. 
54.             // Add the number of events for this trigger to the number of events received in the past
55.             eventCount += (uint)triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count;
56. 
57.             Debug.WriteLine(eventCount + " events processed for lifetime of trigger");
58. 
59.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = eventCount;
60.         }
61.     }

<a id="background-tasks"></a>
## Background Tasks

`IBackgroundTask` / `BackgroundTaskRegistration` are not the recommended model. Use the WinAppSDK [`BackgroundTaskBuilder`](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) (introduced in 1.7), or move the work to push-driven activation / Windows Task Scheduler. See the [background task migration strategy](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).

**If you keep the classic out-of-process model** (`Windows.ApplicationModel.Background.BackgroundTaskBuilder` with a string `TaskEntryPoint` such as `"Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask"` + `builder.Register()`) — which is the low-risk way to preserve existing behavior — then two things are **runtime prerequisites**, not optional:

1. The app must be **packaged** (keep `Package.appxmanifest`), and the **build** manifest must declare an `<Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="<Namespace>.<TaskClass>">` for **every** `TaskEntryPoint` string used in code. This declaration lives in the UWP source manifest — carry it into the WinUI 3 project's root `Package.appxmanifest` (see the manifest checklist below). If it is missing, `builder.Register()` **throws at runtime** (`0x80070032`/class-not-registered) even though the app builds and launches fine.

   ```xml
   <Application ...>
     <Extensions>
       <Extension Category="windows.backgroundTasks" EntryPoint="Tasks.SampleBackgroundTask">
         <BackgroundTasks>
           <Task Type="systemEvent" />
           <Task Type="timer" />
         </BackgroundTasks>
       </Extension>
     </Extensions>
   </Application>
   ```

2. Wrap `Register()` in `try/catch` and surface the failure in the UI (e.g. the Status text). The SDK samples call `Register()` with no guard, so a missing-declaration failure otherwise presents as a **silent dead control** (the button click does nothing, Status never changes) — the hardest kind of regression to notice because there is no build error and no crash.
<shellId: 4 completed with exit code 0>
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueue

UWP (replace this):

```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```

WinUI 3:

```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```

Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<shellId: 3 completed with exit code 0>

32.         {
33.             this.InitializeComponent();
34.             this.Construct();
35.         }

37.         /// <summary>

40.         /// </summary>
41.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>
42.         protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs e)
43.         {
44. 
45. #if DEBUG
46.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
47.             {
48.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = false;
49.             }
50. #endif
51. 
52.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
53. 
54.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
55.             // just ensure that the window is active
56.             if (rootFrame == null)
57.             {
58.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
59.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
60.                 // Set the default language
61.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
62.

64. 
65.                 if (e.PreviousExecutionState == ApplicationExecutionState.Terminated)
66.                 {
67.                     //TODO: Load state from previously suspended application
68.                 }
69. 
70.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
71.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
72.             }
73.

75.             {
76.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
77.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
78.                 // parameter
79.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
80.             }
81.

53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }
163.

113.             {
114.                 // Video casting is not supported by the system.
115.             }

<a id="navigationview-frame-wiring"></a>
### NavigationView + Frame wiring (SDK-sample scenario list)

The UWP SDK-sample idiom `MainPage` + `ListView`/scenario list + `Frame` maps to
`NavigationView` + `Frame` (SKILL.md shell-mapping table). Two **silent** failures are
common — they build and launch with no error, so nothing flags them:

1. **Blank Frame on every page** — a `SelectionChanged` type mismatch (see the wrong-cast
   warning below).
2. **Dead scenarios / first-page-sticks** — the *first* scenario renders but selecting any
   *other* nav item highlights it yet never switches the content `Frame`. This is the more
   dangerous one: it passes an alive-only smoke launch and a "does the first scenario
   render?" check, so it ships broken.

**Do NOT drive navigation from a constructor `SelectedItem` assignment.** Setting
`NavView.SelectedItem` in the constructor (before the item containers are realized) does
**not** reliably raise `SelectionChanged`, and for `MenuItemsSource` data items relying on
`SelectionChanged` alone to drive the `Frame` is unreliable — that is the exact cause of
the dead-scenario bug. Instead: **navigate the first item explicitly on `Loaded`, and
handle `ItemInvoked`** (which fires reliably on every click, for data-bound items too).

**When items come from `MenuItemsSource` (data-bound, e.g. `x:Bind Scenarios`):**
the invoked/selected item is the **bound data item**, NOT a `NavigationViewItem`. Navigate
off the data item's page type:

```csharp
public MainPage()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    Loaded += (_, _) =>                              // explicit initial nav — do NOT rely on SelectedItem
    {
        if (Scenarios.Count > 0)
        {
            NavView.SelectedItem = Scenarios[0];     // highlight only; navigation is explicit below
            ScenarioFrame.Navigate(Scenarios[0].ClassType);
        }
    };
}

private void NavView_ItemInvoked(NavigationView sender, NavigationViewItemInvokedEventArgs args)
{
    if (args.InvokedItem is string || args.InvokedItemContainer?.DataContext is not Scenario)
    {
        // InvokedItem is the bound data item's text; resolve the Scenario from the container.
    }
    if (args.InvokedItemContainer?.DataContext is Scenario s)   // data item, NOT NavigationViewItem
        ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
}
```

Wire `ItemInvoked="NavView_ItemInvoked"` on the `NavigationView` in XAML. `ItemInvoked`
fires on every tap even when re-selecting, which is what keeps the switch reliable.

**When items are literal `NavigationViewItem`s in `MenuItems`:** the invoked item container
*is* a `NavigationViewItem`; read its `Tag`:

```csharp
private void NavView_ItemInvoked(NavigationView sender, NavigationViewItemInvokedEventArgs args)
{
    if (args.InvokedItemContainer is NavigationViewItem item && item.Tag is Type pageType)
        ScenarioFrame.Navigate(pageType);
}
```

Do not mix the two: casting a `MenuItemsSource` selection to `NavigationViewItem` (or
reading `NavView.MenuItems[0]` under `MenuItemsSource`) silently no-ops and yields the
blank-frame bug.

**Falsifiable check (must verify switching, not just first render):** after the shell loads,
the content `Frame` must contain the **first** scenario's controls; then, selecting the
**second** nav item must make the `Frame` show the **second** scenario's controls (its
distinct control names must appear in the UIA tree). If the first scenario renders but
switching to a second nav item leaves scenario-1 controls in the tree, the navigation is
broken — do not ship it.
<shellId: 7 completed with exit code 0>

/// Updates an ObservableCollection based on events from a DeviceWatcher.\n    /// </summary>\n    /// <remarks>\n    /// Encapsulates the work necessary to register for watcher events,\n    /// start and stop the watcher, handle race conditions, and break cycles.\n    /// </remarks>\n    class DeviceWatcherHelper\n    {\n        public DeviceWatcherHelper(\n            ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection,\n            DispatcherQueue dispatcherQueue)\n        {\n            this.resultCollection = resultCollection;\n            this.dispatcherQueue = dispatcherQueue;\n        }\n\n        public delegate void DeviceChangedHandler(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher, string id);\n        public event DeviceChangedHandler DeviceChanged;\n\n        public DeviceWatcher DeviceWatcher => deviceWatcher;\n        public bool UpdateStatus = true;\n\n        public void StartWatcher(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher)\n        {\n            this.deviceWatcher = deviceWatcher;\n\n            // Connect events to update our collection as the watcher report results.\n            deviceWatcher.Added += Watcher_DeviceAdded;\n            deviceWatcher.Updated += Watcher_DeviceUpdated;\n            deviceWatcher.Removed += Watcher_DeviceRemoved;\n            deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted += Watcher_EnumerationCompleted;\n            deviceWatcher.Stopped += Watcher_Stopped;\n\n            deviceWatcher.Start();\n        }\n\n        public void StopWatcher()\n        {\n            // Since the device watcher runs in the background, it is possible that\n            // a notification is \"in flight\" at the time we stop the watcher.\n            // In other words, it is possible for the watcher to become stopped while a\n            // handler is running, or for a handler to run after the watcher has stopped.\n\n            if (IsWatcherStarted(deviceWatcher))\n            {\n                // We do not null out the deviceWatcher yet because we want to receive\n                // the Stopped event.\n                deviceWatcher.Stop();\n            }\n        }\n\n        public void Reset()\n        {\n            if (deviceWatcher != null)\n            {\n                StopWatcher();\n                deviceWatcher = null;\n            }\n        }\n\n        DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;\n        ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection;\n        DispatcherQueue dispatcherQueue;\n\n        static bool IsWatcherStarted(DeviceWatcher watcher)\n        {\n            return (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||\n                (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted);\n        }\n\n        public bool IsWatcherRunning()\n        {\n            if (deviceWatcher == null)\n            {\n                return false;\n            }\n\n            DeviceWatcherStatus status = deviceWatcher.Status;\n            return (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||\n                (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted) ||\n                (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopping);\n        }\n\n        private void Watcher_DeviceAdded(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformation deviceInfo)\n        {\n            // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.\n            dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.Low, () =>\n            {\n                // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.\n                if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))\n                {\n                    resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(deviceInfo));\n                    RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfo.Id);\n                }\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void Watcher_DeviceUpdated(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)\n        {\n            // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.\n            dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.Low, () =>\n            {\n                // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.\n                if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))\n                {\n                    // Find the corresponding updated DeviceInformation in the collection and pass the update object\n                    // to the Update method of the existing DeviceInformation. This automatically updates the object\n                    // for us.\n                    foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)\n                    {\n                        if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)\n                        {\n                            deviceInfoDisp.Update(deviceInfoUpdate);\n                            RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);\n                            break;\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void Watcher_DeviceRemoved(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)\n        {\n            // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.\n            dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.Low, () =>\n            {\n                // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.\n                if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))\n                {\n                    // Find the corresponding DeviceInformation in the collection and remove it\n                    foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)\n                    {\n                        if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)\n                        {\n                            resultCollection.Remove(deviceInfoDisp);\n                            break;\n                        }\n                    }\n\n                    RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);\n                }\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void Watcher_EnumerationCompleted(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)\n        {\n            dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.Low, () =>\n            {\n                RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void Watcher_Stopped(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)\n        {\n            dispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.Low, () =>\n            {\n                RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);\n            });\n        }\n\n        private void RaiseDeviceChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)\n        {\n            if (UpdateStatus)\n            {\n                // Display a default status message.\n                string message = string.Empty;\n                switch (sender.Status)\n                {\n                    case DeviceWatcherStatus.Started:\n                        message = $\"{resultCollection.Count} devices found.\";\n                        break;\n\n                    case DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted:\n                        message = $\"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Enumeration completed. Watching for updates...\";\n                        break;\n\n                    case DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopped:\n                        message = $\"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher stopped.\";\n                        break;\n\n                    case DeviceWatcherStatus.Aborted:\n                        message = $\"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher aborted.\";\n                        break;\n                }\n\n                if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(message))\n                {\n                    MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(message, NotifyType.StatusMessage);\n                }\n            }\n\n            DeviceChanged?.Invoke(sender, id);\n        }\n    }\n}\n","old_str":"// Copyright (c) Microsoft. Response comes back and we set it on the PinComparePairingRequestedData\n                    // then complete the deferral.\n                    var displayMessageDeferral = args.GetDeferral();\n\n                    await DispatcherQueue.EnqueueAsync(async () =>\n                    {\n                        bool accept = await GetUserConfirmationAsync(args.Pin);\n                        if (accept)\n                        {\n                            args.Accept();\n                        }\n\n                        displayMessageDeferral.Complete();\n                    });\n                    break;\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void ShowPairingPanel(string text, DevicePairingKinds pairingKind)\n        {\n            pairingPanel.Visibility = 20.         public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
21.         {
22.             Debug.WriteLine("BackgroundDeviceWatcher.Run called");
23. 
24.             uint eventCount = 0;
25.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
26.             DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails triggerDetails = (DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
27. 
28.             Debug.WriteLine(String.Format("Trigger contains {0} events", triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count));
29. 
30.             foreach (DeviceWatcherEvent e in triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents)
31.             {
32.                 switch (e.Kind)
33.                 {
34.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add:
35.                     Debug.WriteLine("Add: " + e.DeviceInformation.Id);
36.                     break;
37. 
38.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update:
39.                     Debug.WriteLine("Update: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
40.                     break;
41. 
42.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove:
43.                     Debug.WriteLine("Remove: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             };
47.             
48.             if (null != settings.Values["eventCount"] &&
49.                 settings.Values["eventCount"].GetType() == typeof(uint))
50.             {
51.                 eventCount = (uint)settings.Values["eventCount"];
52.             }
53. 
54.             // Add the number of events for this trigger to the number of events received in the past
55.             eventCount += (uint)triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count;
56. 
57.             Debug.WriteLine(eventCount + " events processed for lifetime of trigger");
58. 
59.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = eventCount;
60.         }

59.         {
60.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
61.             resultCollection.Clear();
62. 
63.             // Get the device selector chosen by the UI then add additional constraints for devices that
64.             // can be paired or are already paired.
65.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
66.             string selector = "(" + deviceSelectorInfo.Selector + ")" + " AND (System.Devices.Aep.CanPair:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True OR System.Devices.Aep.IsPaired:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True)";
67. 
68.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher;
69.             if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
70.             {
71.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
72.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
73.                     selector,
74.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
75.                     );
76.             }
77.             else
78.             {
79.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
80.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
81.                     selector,
82.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
83.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
84.             }
85. 
86.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
87.             deviceWatcherHelper.StartWatcher(deviceWatcher);
88.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
89.         }

92.         {
93.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
94.

96.

98.         }

100.         private void OnDeviceListChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
101.         {
102.             // If the item being updated is currently "selected", then update the pairing buttons
103.             DeviceInformationDisplay selectedDeviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;
104.             if ((selectedDeviceInfoDisp != null) && (selectedDeviceInfoDisp.Id == id))

107.             }

111.         {
112.             // Gray out the pair button and results view while pairing is in progress.
113.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
114.             pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
115.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Pairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
116. 
117.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
118. 
119.             DevicePairingResult dpr = await deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.PairAsync();
120. 
121.             rootPage.NotifyUser(
122.                 "Pairing result = " + dpr.Status.ToString(),
123.                 dpr.Status == DevicePairingResultStatus.Paired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
124. 
125.             UpdatePairingButtons();
126.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;
127.         }

133.             unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;

135. 
136.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
137. 
138.             DeviceUnpairingResult dupr = await deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.UnpairAsync();
139. 
140.             rootPage.NotifyUser(
141.                 "Unpairing result = " + dupr.Status.ToString(),
142.                 dupr.Status == DeviceUnpairingResultStatus.Unpaired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
143. 
144.             UpdatePairingButtons();
145.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void ResultsListView_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
149.         {
150.             UpdatePairingButtons();
151.         }
152. 
153.         private void UpdatePairingButtons()
154.         {
155.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;
156. 
157.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
158.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.CanPair &&
159.                 !deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
160.             {
161.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = true;
162.             }
163.             else
164.             {
165.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
166.             }
167. 
168.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
169.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
170.             {
171.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = true;
172.             }
173.             else
174.             {
175.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;
176.             }

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates

- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.

### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:

- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.

Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.

### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually have

The WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).

If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:

```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```

(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)

You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:

- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.

- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.

Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires runFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capability>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matter what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern).

5. **Carry over every UWP `<Extension>` the app's behavior depends on.** The scaffold manifest has none; the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` are activation/registration prerequisites — omitting them makes the feature fail **at runtime** (often as a silently dead control), not at build time. Copy each `<Extension>` block from the UWP source manifest (under `.uwp-source/` or the copied UWP `Package.appxmanifest`) into the WinUI 3 root manifest's `<Application>`. Common ones:

   | Extension `Category` | Feature it powers |
   |----------------------|-------------------|
   | `windows.backgroundTasks` | out-of-process `BackgroundTaskBuilder` tasks (one `<Extension EntryPoint="...">` per `TaskEntryPoint`) |
   | `windows.protocol` | custom URI-scheme activation |
   | `windows.fileTypeAssociation` | open-with / file activation |
   | `windows.appService` | background app services |
   | `windows.shareTarget` | receiving shared content |

   `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` FAILs if retained `BackgroundTaskBuilder` code has no `windows.backgroundTasks` extension; the other categories have no automated check, so verify them by diffing the UWP manifest's `<Extensions>` against the migrated one.

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Threading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Search for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires ALT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalent requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code constants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Migrating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites just the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHelper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavigationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, the LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

=== TURN 89 ===
The background task extension needs the activatable class registration. For WinUI3 packaged apps with out-of-process background tasks, the task class must be registered as an activatable class. Let me check the migration pattern guidance again - it says "the declaration lives in the UWP source manifest". The issue is that for Win32 desktop apps, the BackgroundTask with `IBackgroundTask` interface requires an activatable class inprocess server, OR the background task has to be declared differently.

For WinUI3 desktop apps, out-of-process background tasks need an `inProcessServer` extension instead. But actually, the simplest fix here is to remove the background task extension (or comment it out) since the background task runs in a separate process and this would require a whole separate project. Per the migration pattern, the background task code is kept but the manifest extension is what causes the issue.

Actually the real problem is that for Win32/WinUI3 desktop apps, in-process background tasks must be declared via `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer`. The background task runs inside the host process. Let me update the manifest:The background task extension needs the activatable class registration. For WinUI3 packaged apps with out-of-process background tasks, the task class must be registered as an activatable class. Let me check the migration pattern guidance again - it says "the declaration lives in the UWP source manifest". The issue is that for Win32 desktop apps, the BackgroundTask with `IBackgroundTask` interface requires an activatable class inprocess server, OR the background task has to be declared differently.

For WinUI3 desktop apps, out-of-process background tasks need an `inProcessServer` extension instead. But actually, the simplest fix here is to remove the background task extension (or comment it out) since the background task runs in a separate process and this would require a whole separate project. Per the migration pattern, the background task code is kept but the manifest extension is what causes the issue.

Actually the real problem is that for Win32/WinUI3 desktop apps, in-process background tasks must be declared via `windows.activatableClass.inProcessServer`. The background task runs inside the host process. Let me update the manifest:
